ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u59	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The legislative act under procedure introduces a certain order to the Press Law of 1984. On behalf of the Kukiz 15 Club, I present my attitude to this law. This order applies to the application of the rules on the authorisation of the statements of journalists. The most important change is that the journalist must inform the person giving information about his right to authorize a literally quoted statement. The person wishing to avail himself of this must make such a request without delay. Nor will the journalist be able to refuse authorization, but he will not have to obtain it when it is public. This will certainly make it easier for journalists to work, but it will also provide protection for information providers. The legislative act introduces regulation as to the nature of the substantive and formal authorisation itself. Due to the needs of modern interpersonal communication, quite strict terms of authorisation are introduced. This obviously makes sense - to get the information to the public as soon as possible. It is also beneficial to add that the lack of authorisation will not be a crime and will be treated as a misdemeanor. The Act is also introducing cosmetic changes due to the situation that takes place after 1989 in Poland. These are e.g.: removal from the Act of naming directly related to the Polish People's Republic, repeal of the provisions concerning the obligation of the journalist to carry out the programming line of the letter in which he is employed, an indication - quite obvious - that the editor-in-chief of the letter cannot be the person convicted of the heaviest crimes against the interests of Poland, the elimination of an unnecessary body such as the Press Council. The revision despite such changes is superficial. The removal of terminology from the communist state does not change in any way the oppressive nature of the Press Law Act. After all, it was adopted to ensure that the totalitarian state was supervised by insubordinate journalists and a certain formatting of the public's awareness of the press content. And it is known to be used as such. Moreover, in more than 30 years of its operation, there have been new developments concerning the press market. These circumstances include, for example, technological developments related to the acquisition of information by journalists, changes in the awareness of journalists and audiences as regards the functional nature of the press information. Unfortunately, in the Press Law amended by the current legislative act, nothing has changed in this respect. The obligations of public information providers have not been defined. This is a serious pathology that is being used. For example, in Krakow, the entity carrying out a public research project made it impossible to obtain information so-called. a social journalist, considering that his intellectual product, I remind, financed by public funds, is private property. In democratic countries with a greater understanding of civil institutions' involvement, e.g. in the United States, no one would think of such an attitude, because there is a legal culture and a service role of bodies using public funds and providing information to a journalist asking about it is a culture of a free state. And in Poland it is the culture of a country enslaved, also by press law reaching the roots of communist totalitarianism. There is such a psychic situation that an expert in a public seminar, referring to his personal interests, does not allow a journalist to publicize his image or voice, because he feels bad when someone hears that voice. The existing Press Law in Poland serves to kill, or at least hinder the functioning of Internet journalism. This violates civil rights, but also public and public interests. Changes related to this Press Law Act should also be included in other legislation, e.g. Criminal code. The existing Press Law provides very limited protection for the journalist. I believe that, in the end, the scope of the Article should be clearly defined. 212 Penal Code. I mean, he's the one that makes you race journalists. We have an example of punishing journalists for a word, which is to describe the real events that in the local notepad's sense are in his honor. That's the way it is. in Wadowice, where a local blogger is being prosecuted by the mayor. This undermines the freedom of the media and leads to the intimidation of insubordinate journalists. To sum up, [[♪ The bell ♪]] The Kukiz'15 club is in favour of the bill, but points to the need to completely remodel it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u65	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on government and commission drafts of laws amending the Press Law Act. The project removes outdated concepts such as: Polish People's Republic, and non-existent government administrations, i.e. National councils and press councils. The last Press Council was established in 1985 and operated until 1988 and was never established again. It is not justified that, after so many years since the change in the political system, we should continue to refer to the rules of the Polish People's Republic. The present law is a fundamental legal act governing the activity of the press, which refers to provisions guaranteeing freedom of expression. There cannot be such a situation that the provisions of the Act refer to non-existent institutions in today's reality. According to the Court, the provisions of the present law give unlimited right to interviewers to prevent journalists from publishing the information they have heard. This may have a major impact on the failure of journalists to raise more in-depth questions for fear of refusal of authorisation. In addition, the disadvantage of the current law is the lack of time limits in which the right to authorisation can be exercised. The proposed bill introduces detailed authorisation regulations, among others. a journalist will not have to obtain authorization in the event of a public statement. The person giving the statement who wishes to exercise his right will be obliged to report this fact to the journalist, the journalist being obliged to inform the person giving the information about his right. The new provisions of the Act will introduce a time limit for authorisation. If the person giving the interview does not carry out an authorisation within the statutory deadline, it will mean consent to the publication of a literally quoted statement. The new rules repeal the existing criminal sanctions and introduce criminal liability in place. The penalties were disproportionate in relation to the goods which were protected by the authorisation law. During the committee's work, two convergent draft laws were discussed from print No. 1604 - government project and No. 1793 - draft petition committee. The Commission decided to make use of the law and convergence as regards regulations, to consider the projects together and to include them in print No 1604, taking into account the amendment of the Commission's draft, which concerns the replacement of national councils with the identification of the relevant local government units. The rules introduced will facilitate the work of journalists, reduce responsibility and prevent the use of the right of authorisation to block the press. The earlier provisions of the Act could have had a fundamental impact on the delay in the flow of relevant information from a social point of view. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding the bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Thus, while in 2013 the funds allocated to accident prevention support were at the level of 300 000. zł, in 2016, the size of the measures taken by employers to prevent accidents at work and occupational diseases reached the level of PLN 55 million. In addition, the Act sets out the procedure, rules and procedure for the cancellation of competitions for contributors who apply for funding for actions aimed at maintaining their working capacity throughout their working life. Another element is the conditions that the payer must fulfil in order to apply for funding under the competition. The Act also regulates the process of identifying an entity assisting the Social Insurance Institution in the implementation of accident prevention and lays down rules for its cooperation with the Social Insurance Institution. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u91	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I am pleased to present a report by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the Government's draft law on amending the Act on Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund and the Act on Social Insurance for Accidents at Work and Occupational Diseases, contained in print No. 1817. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Several years ago, i.e. in July 2005, the Sejm adopted an amendment to the Act on pensions from the Social Insurance Fund. By virtue of its provisions from January 2006, persons who have been in an incapacity for work pension and have reached retirement age during that period have become eligible for retirement without the need to submit any additional, appropriate application. In the case of these ex officio pensions, where there was no required traineeship, only the amount of the old-age pension was guaranteed. This means that this pension may not be lower than the old-age pension for incapacity for work, but that pension has not been covered by the guarantee of the amount of the lowest benefits. It's been like this for years. Annual values weren't very high, so maybe that's why this situation hasn't been taken into account. From 1 March 2017, the lowest pension, provided that you have the required internship, i.e. for women 20 years or 22 years - the difference is that in 2012 the previous coalition adopted a law which entered into force since January 2013 and this law not only extended the retirement age but also extended the required traineeship for women and so systematically we have now reached 22 years of the required internship... I am happy to say that from 1 October we are back to our internship, as we are to the lower retirement age for women, and also to the 20-year internship and - on the subject - 25 years for men. The total incapacity and survivor's pensions were raised from PLN 882,56 to PLN 1000, while the partial incapacity pensions were raised from PLN 676,75 to PLN 750. The government draft law, which entered the Sejm on 9 August this year, proposes that ex officio pensions also be covered by the guarantee of the amount of the lowest benefit, regardless of whether the pensioner has the required internship, had, at the time of retirement from office, or did not have, respectively equal to PLN 1000 - this corresponds to the amount of the lowest pension for total incapacity for work, or PLN 750 - this corresponds to the lowest pension for partial incapacity for work, depending on whether the pension is converted for total or partial incapacity for work, i.e. Article 2 Point 1 (3) of the proposed law. Personally, I'm very happy with this project, and I think it's not just me, because in March this year. Many pensioners began to claim that, despite the government's announcement, they did not get a raise in this pension to PLN 1000. Several people came to my office whose pensions did not reach the amount declared by the government. There was even a person - I couldn't believe it, but I saw a pension receipt - whose pension is less than PLN 600. That's why it's a joy that this project changes that. This project contains one more point. Another solution proposed in the project is to organise the rules for the granting of pensions for incapacity for work. A provision shall be made to prevent the right to an invalidity pension from being obtained by a person who has previously been entitled to a pension or by a person who has reached his retirement age. The bill assumes a 14-day vacathio legis, but the valorization will be paid next year, together with next year's valorization, however, I would like to stress, with an equalization from March this year. The Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy carried out its first reading and discussed this proposal at its meeting on 13 September. And she accepted it unanimously. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy recommends the adoption of a draft law without amendments. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u107	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to thank you for your unanimity as regards the support of the Government's draft law on amending the Social Insurance Fund pension law, and to thank all parliamentary clubs. I think we all see the need to amend the rules discussed today. It can be said that the history of the amended legislation dates back to 2005, when a new legal institution was introduced into the Polish social security system - the institution of retirement from office by the person entitled to the pension for incapacity to work with the achievement of the retirement age. This is an exceptional solution because it is an exception to the principle that the right to a specific social security benefit is determined at the request of the insured person. In this case, the right to a pension shall be determined ex officio by decision of the pension authority. It can be said that it is an institution which is already in force 12 years after the law amending the Social Insurance Fund pension law entered into force. During the discussion on this project, several times the question was why it was so late. I'll try to answer that question. In fact, the need to revise the rules governing the transition from the pensioner's status to the pensioner's status results from existing or existing arrangements for the valorisation of pension benefits. It can be said that we have so far applied the principle that pension benefits are valued, increased by a percentage rate taking into account the price increase of consumer goods and services and the share of the wage growth rate, including the so-called "wage growth rate." the valorisation index. According to the valorisation rate or the level of valorisation, the amounts of the lowest pension benefits, the lowest incapacity for work and the lowest pensions have been raised so far. The phenomenon, the problem or the legal gap that you mentioned in the course of the discussion really made itself known on 1 March 2017, when an unprecedented operation to increase the amount of the lowest pension benefits in the general social security system was carried out so far. As long as the amounts of the lowest pension benefits increased as if according to the valorisation rate, it could be said that these amounts followed precisely the price increase rate of consumer goods and services, taking into account this wage element. On 1 March 2017, the lowest pension benefits were increased from PLN 882 to PLN 1 000. PLN in the case of pensions and total incapacity pension and up to PLN 750 in the case of partial incapacity pension. It can be said that this guarantee, which was enshrined in the Act, guarantee that the old-age pension will not be lower than the amount of the pension for incapacity for work previously collected, has been insufficient for those persons who have failed to reach the required contribution and non-contributory period of 22 years for women and 25 years for men today, but since 1 October following the entry into force of the Law reducing or restoring the previously applicable retirement age of only 20 years for women and 25 years for men. It turned out that the group was about 105 thousand. recipients are not covered by this guarantee to obtain at least the lowest pension benefit, even though they were covered by the guarantee to obtain the lowest pension for incapacity to work. Hence, the initiative, the idea to amend the law and, you can say, extend the circle of people covered by the guarantee to obtain the lowest pension benefit just for those who previously charged the pension for incapacity for work and did not achieve this minimum period of insurance. This is one element of the bill discussed today. The second element is actually similar in nature, that is to say it complements a lack, a deficiency or a legal gap in the social security system since 2005. In fact, the intention to amend the Pension Act at that time in 2005 was to eliminate the possibility of taking an invalidity pension after reaching the retirement age. This is what this institution of transition from the status of a pensioner to the status of a pensioner was intended to serve. One could say that one problem was not noticed. Namely, it was not included in the provisions of the amended pension law at that time, which would make it impossible to apply for an invalidity pension to a person who reached the retirement age, i.e. a mechanism for the transition from a pensioner's status to a pensioner's status, but no rules were introduced which would limit the possibility of applying for an invalidity pension by a person who has already reached the retirement age. As far as this element is concerned, it is also an attempt to answer the question of why today we are discussing change and the elimination of the current lack of regulation. So this is the element that results from the pension review that was carried out in 2016. The results of this pension review were presented to the High House and one of the elements advocated in this pension review concerns this second element of today's draft law. Once again, I thank all parliamentary clubs for their support for the government bill and ask the High House to vote in favour of this project. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u113	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On 12 January this year, Eurostat reported that Bermuda was the third country in terms of the volume of directly absorbed foreign investments of the European Union, and it was EUR 353 billion. The first place was the United States with more than EUR 2.5 trillion and the second was Switzerland with EUR 829 billion. On the other hand, in terms of the volume of foreign direct investment that entered the European Union, the United States held first place - EUR 2436 billion, Switzerland second place - EUR 627 billion, and Bermuda third again with EUR 503 billion. These numbers show where we are after Panama Papers. After all these scandals, tax havens are still a widely used way to avoid taxes. The OECD estimates that the tax avoidance loss is between USD 100 and 240 billion per year. Of course, the poorest lose the most, which is even more aggravating the inequality. Today, the 10 richest people have more wealth than the poorer half of mankind, and 1% The richest have more assets than 99% The others. At the same time Apple in Ireland pays 0,005% tax. The principle that taxes are paid by small and medium-sized enterprises, and international corporations optimize taxes, privatising profits and socialising losses, such as. banks during the global crisis, notabene the same banks that make the fourth part of Europe's income in tax havens, is - as Vice Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki has said many times - a real hell for societies, budgets and states. In short, what is the mechanism of tax havens? Or we set up in a tax paradise a company to which we put our assets or e.g. the remuneration that is not taxed or taxed minimally, either we transfer money to companies in tax havens or sell goods by a company registered in a tax haven, thus avoiding tax in the country of our business. It can therefore be said that any step towards restoring the principle that companies pay tax where they make profits, where they actually do business, is a step in the right direction. Therefore, a multilateral convention signed on 7 June 2017 in Paris, so-called. The Multilateral Instrument, MLI, developed in the framework of the OECD and G20 BEPS project, is called by many experts a revolution in double taxation agreements. Poland is among the first 68 signatory countries and has been involved in this project from the very beginning. The Convention has now been signed by 71 countries and another six have indicated their willingness to sign the Convention in the near future. In total, more than 111 tax jurisdictions joined the ad hoc group that worked on the creation of the Convention. Almost all countries of the European Union are among the countries that have signed the Convention. The Convention has not yet been signed by Estonia - only signed a letter of intent. The Convention has already been ratified by the first country, Austria. We have a chance to be the second country to ratify this convention. Today, therefore, I have the honour to present to the High House a report of the Joint Committees: the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Foreign Affairs on the Government's draft Act on the ratification of the multilateral Convention implementing measures of Treaty tax law aimed at preventing the erosion of the tax base and the transfer of profits, drawn up in Paris on 24 November 2016, print No 1776. The joint committees are asking the Sejm to adopt a draft law without amendment. In the preamble proposed by the Convention, which constitutes its so-called "preamble." the minimum standard, the parties have written: "In view of the need to ensure that existing double taxation agreements are interpreted in order to eliminate double taxation in respect of taxes covered by those agreements without creating the possibility of non-taxation or reduced taxation by tax evasion or avoidance (including through the acquisition of contractual advantages by unauthorised persons, treat-shopping, with the aim of obtaining the benefits provided for in those agreements indirectly for the benefit of persons resident or established in third jurisdictions); "seeing the need to create an effective mechanism for implementing the agreed changes in a synchronised and effective manner within the network of existing double taxation agreements, without the need to bilaterally renegotiate each of these agreements (...) ', so much preamble. This means that avoiding double taxation must not mean tax avoidance at all and that the only way to ensure a certain standard at the same time in many countries and to change nearly 3 000. bilateral agreements have not lasted for the next 100 years, the best solution will be the Convention. Four areas were selected from several OECD reports on the necessary changes in tax law sealing systems, i.e.: hybrid structures, treaty abuse, artificial avoidance of the establishment of a foreign person and improvement of dispute resolution mechanisms. And that was the MLI convention. At this stage, the OECD estimates that it changes around 1100 contracts, of which Poland has notified to change 70 bilateral agreements. The Convention gives individual countries the opportunity to choose the extent to which it is adopted. And here Poland is ready to adopt the Convention to a very wide extent. Only, of course, it takes two to tango and so on. Poland indicated an agreement with Kuwait, but Kuwait did not. Such agreements, where we have the consent of the other party, Poland has 46 at the moment. It is also worth noting that although the Convention is superior, it does not replace bilateral agreements and will be applied either instead of bilateral agreements, or it will modify them or act directly in the absence of bilateral agreements. The Convention gives certain options and provides for mandatory provisions. The mandatory provision is to adopt a solution in the form of a PPT test - the principal purpose test - which means that the introduction of the PPT clause into the contractual provisions will prevent the use of the treaty provisions from being misused and used in aggressive tax planning. The clause provides for the absence of an advantage to the Treaty where the acquisition of these benefits was one of the main objectives of creating structures and concluding transactions. The adoption of the clause is a minimum standard and will therefore be introduced into all agreements with States that accede to the Convention, including those whose jurisdictions are abused for aggressive optimization. An example of this effect is the possibility to challenge the absence of taxation or the application of a lower rate of withholding tax, in Poland on dividends, interest, royalties paid to a foreign company, if one of the main purposes of the structure was to use the favourable provisions of the agreement. The option adopted by Poland is e.g. Real estate clause. The introduction of a real estate clause will allow taxation in Poland of the proceeds from the sale of shares or shares where the assets are mostly composed of properties located in Poland. An example of this effect is the possibility of taxation in Poland of income from the sale of shares or shares in the company by its foreign partner, if the assets of the company consist of more than 50% real estate located in Poland. Other options chosen by Poland include: regulations on: tax transparent entities - restricting the use of hybrid non-compliances, double-established entities - restricting the abuse of contracts, sealing the use of double taxation methods, sealing the collection of withholding tax on dividends or reservations on the right to tax own residents. These records form a whole with successive periodic warnings from the Minister of Finance, for example.: avoidance of taxes on income generated by closed investment funds, fictitious returns used to increase the value of the company and earn on depreciation write-offs, but above all before the fictitious management established abroad a capital company then used for tax optimization. The Convention also complements the tax evasion clause introduced by the government by Prime Minister Beata Szydło to the National Tax Order. When will the convention come into force? Assuming ratification and other procedures required by internal law by at least five States acceding to the Convention in October, November this year, we have 3 full months to enter into force of the Convention. However, the Convention will apply from the following year, i.e. the first amendments to tax agreements will have an effect at the earliest beginning of 2019. The very way in which the Convention enters into force is flexible, because countries still have the opportunity to change, until ratification and the extension of the Convention and after its adoption, and complicated, because other deadlines apply for withholding tax and others for other taxes. The ratification of the Convention by both parties is required to amend the existing double taxation agreement. Poland, as I said, is one of the first countries that have already undertaken ratification procedures. Finally, the concerns of Poles in the UK. We know about the petition that was made and which is addressed to the government. Although the convention concerns, once again I will highlight, large international companies, it raises doubts among Poles working in the UK. It is therefore worth noting that Poles who moved abroad together with their families and work there are not subject to tax in Poland at all. This means that if their center of life interests, i.e. family and economic connection is abroad, they will not pay tax on foreign income in Poland. On the other hand, if someone goes to work abroad only for a short time and returns to Poland, where e.g. His family lives, it may be subject to taxation in Poland, but it may benefit from a national provision called abolition relief, which brings the tax to the same amount as it was before the amendments resulting from the Convention. Generally speaking, the convention is about preventing people from taxing twice, i.e. paying no tax either in the UK or in Poland. After the ratification of the MLI by Poland and Great Britain, any doubts will be explained to Poles in the Islands, and I can assure you that in nine cases in 10 cases the Convention will have no impact on Poles working in the UK. Once again, it is about reducing the aggressive tax optimizations of the largest economic operators in Europe and the world, not making life difficult for Poles abroad in any way. Thank you very much for the opportunity to report on behalf of the Joint Commission: Public Finance and Foreign Affairs. [[Applause]]

POZ can and should play a key role in the system, it should be the foundation of this system and has both the predisposition and the need for potential. However, this need for reform, which stems from the proposal presented to you, refers to the lack of appropriate regulation to strengthen the role of this level in the healthcare system. Currently, issues relating to POS are regulated in a number of different legal acts, which is why new solutions are justified for the development of this area, which will allow to redefine the role and place of POZ in the health system. Most importantly, the reform will create mechanisms for continuous improvement of the quality and safety of the benefits provided, including the organisation of the functioning of the POS and as a result of the whole system. At the moment, this role is marginalized with a loss to both the patient and the healthcare system. The aim of the reform that we want to implement through this draft legislation contained in print 1813 is to strengthen this part of the system and, importantly, to create the conditions for improving the effectiveness of basic healthcare. We want to make an even greater contribution to preserving and improving the health of our country's society, and the benefits for citizens are to include among others. improve the satisfaction of the care provided, increase the availability of benefits and improve the quality and safety of the benefits provided. In particular, it will be very important to improve the coordination of care for patients. The aim of the proposed law is to introduce a model of patient-centred care and health needs, to increase the role of prevention and health education, to coordinate and integrate treatment at different levels of medical care, to optimise the treatment process, to create a mechanism enabling financial incentives for healthcare providers to continuously improve the quality of their services. The key objective of the reform is to set up POZ teams. Every patient in Poland, every citizen of our country will be taken care of by such a team, including a doctor, nurse and midwife. The team's work will be coordinated by a doctor who will determine how and how to perform diagnostic and therapeutic measures on the patient. However, members of the team will work on the basis of the principle of professional independence of their members. We can in part, in many places of our country see such a situation already at the moment, but we know that it is only the most common care or form of care in which these three entities have a lot to show, so to speak, good will to show that such coordinated care within this team is offered to the patient. We want to create real conditions for such cooperation, for the constant exchange of information and comprehensive patient care. The team is to be the primary tool for primary healthcare. The Act does not impose any restrictions on the form of organizational and legal functioning of teams. The possibility of different medicinal entities, such as entrepreneurs, natural or legal persons, for example, to operate in the area of POZ will be maintained. SPOZ, non-governmental organisations, and apprenticeships such as individual and group practices, medical, nursing or midwives' practices. In all the activities carried out by the Ministry, we want to make coordination a key principle for the operation of this system. The ideas of health care in recent years have been based on the principle that money follows the patient. Today we already know that this is not really, first of all, true, and it is not quite an effective method to build this system effectively. However, before we knew it, the care system was quite fragmented and today it consists of a number of autonomous units, each of which treats patients suffering from a different disease and on different terms. That is why we want to restore the coordination of healthcare with the patient in the health system. The pillar of this coordination, the patient's guardian will be POZ. It is at this elementary level that the doctor should direct the patient, as appropriate, to the appropriate area in the healthcare system. The draft law introduces a model of patient coordination, including the use of ICT solutions. The intention of this model is to ensure continuity and comprehensiveness of care, so that the patient remains healthy as long as possible. The coordinator, and in many cases the patient's guide in the health care system will be the doctor of the POZ working with the nurse and midwife. A great deal of emphasis will be placed on the cooperation of primary health care with other parts of the system, in particular with outpatient specialised care. There will also be an emphasis on the exchange of information on the patient's health, e.g. in terms of diagnosis, treatment, prognosis, or ordained medicines - the possibility of introducing a prescription will then be much easier - or the appointment of inspection visits. Within the framework of coordination, patients will be provided with preventive care appropriate to their age and gender, as well as diagnostic studies and specialist consultations in accordance with individual diagnostic plans for a particular patient, a prepared treatment for him and comprehensive care. The draft law requires each provider to designate a so-called provider. an administrative coordinator to coordinate the organisation of the process of providing health services, including the provision of information to the patient on this process. The introduction of a coordinated care model will be preceded by a pilot programme which will be implemented by 31 December 2019 under the so-called provisions. pilot law, whose project you have in sejm print No 1791. The new POZ we want to rely to a large extent on medical specialisation, which will be related to this level of care, i.e. family medicine, which we supplement with pediatricians as well. Doctors with the title of specialist in family medicine will be the strength of the new POZ. However, conscious of the challenges facing us we do not close ourselves to other specializations: the doctor of POZ is also a doctor with specializations in general medicine or in the field of internal diseases. This is what we will keep for a few more years so that all - and doctors and patients - will have time to adapt to the new conditions of the basic health care system. Like the safest Western European countries, such as England, the Netherlands, Norway, Denmark, we want to shift the task of coordinating treatment to a family doctor. These changes require preparation, doctors are needed for these specializations and well-educated medical staff, so we want to achieve the desired condition after 1 January 2025. In the end, a primary health care doctor will be a specialist in family medicine, a doctor during such a specialization, a specialist in general medicine, a specialist in first or second degree medicine or a doctor who is a specialist in the field of pediatrics. Importantly, the right to work in POZ will also be preserved by doctors who are in this system or will be in it before 1 January 2025, among others. Internists who provided benefits just until 31 December 2024 and doctors who have rights acquired under the provisions of the Law on the provision of public-funded health care. The same approach was adopted for the nurse and midwife of the POZ, specifying as the target qualifications the completion or completion of specialization training or the qualification course in the field of family nursing or having a professional degree in nursing and maternity respectively. At the same time, due to the shortage of staff specialising in family nursing, it was assumed that nurses and midwives of the POZ would be respectively nurses and midwives meeting the previous qualification requirements - in this area there is a broad catalogue of qualification courses and specialized training - which will provide health services in primary healthcare before 31 December 2024. One of the priorities of the whole health service, but also of this level of care, i.e. primary healthcare, is the implementation of solutions to improve the quality of services and the creation of mechanisms for monitoring this quality. These actions will be implemented systematically in every area, especially in those that we are undergoing a thorough reform, such as the POZ. The proposed regulation introduces an obligation to monitor the quality of the benefits provided, including inter alia. obtain health effects, availability, complexity and continuity of the benefits provided. In addition, it enables providers to participate in quality improvement programmes. As far as funding is concerned, funding in basic health care will thus far be provided by the NFZ's financial plan. The financial plan for 2017 shows that PLN 9.9 billion is planned for basic health care, which is 13.5% expenditure planned for benefits. As we extend the scope of benefits, we also assume a progressive annual increase in expenditure on the functioning of this part of the health system. The current POZ funding system does not encourage active attitudes by healthcare providers and coordination of both prevention and treatment of patients. We are setting up a new way of financing, which is to be a incentive system, which should ensure that the resources allocated to health services are used optimally. The basic health care funding will be based on a capital charge. In addition to it, there will be a budget allocated, i.e. the resources allocated to the provision of coordinated care, a task fee, i.e. additional resources allocated to the provision of preventive health care, as well as an incentive supplement for the outcome of treatment and the quality of care provided to the patient. We want to introduce further reform assumptions gradually so that each of the changes will work fully from the date indicated. We don't want to wish for a bill that becomes a dead letter of the law. Therefore, the next stages of the reform will be introduced in the following years, when all or rather the vast majority of the participants in the system will be prepared accordingly. From 2018, we will launch a pilot programme, coordinated care and a new funding system will start from 2020, and family medicine will become a pillar of primary health care from 2025. In order to best show the whole of the changes that the proposed law is implementing in basic healthcare, it is worth to follow the patient's steps in this system and in this designed one. Currently, POZ is only a place of emergency intervention in the case of the disease - and this is most often the result of this level of care - while in the proposed system thanks to this act we want to make POZ a place to ensure not only emergency intervention of the doctor, but also prevention and health education. Currently in this individual approach POZ doctors and POZ nurses are independent of each other, i.e. treatment of the patient is reduced to uncoordinated and ad hoc activities. In a system where the primary healthcare team operates, we propose extensive cooperation - and we will give these opportunities - between the doctor, nurse and midwife, which will mean the treatment of the patient based on individual therapeutic, diagnostic, health plans for each patient requiring such care. Currently, the patient is forced to refer to the so-called. a real specialist doctor, but we want the patient to receive a package of studies and specialist consultations in the proposed system, which will be coordinated by the family doctor in consultation with the specialist. Currently, a patient who has received referrals is lonely in search of healthcare providers. We want the proposed POZ system to coordinate further management of the diagnostic and therapeutic process by providing information and organizational support to the patient. Currently, the patient disappears from the field of view of POZ after a complete diagnosis or treatment. In the future, the POZ team will cooperate with other parts of the health system, pass on information about the patient and constantly take care of him. The planned date of entry into force of the first proposed solutions is 1 December 2017. We are prepared for this at the Ministry of Health, so we ask for active work in the Sejm. At the same time, we are still open to suggestions and proposals on the practical functioning of the new basic health care, which is why the government has submitted Self-correction to the bill, which proposes maintaining the current legal status, which allows the doctor to provide health services to children and young people until they reach the age of 18. In addition, the amendments proposed in the self-correction oblige pediatricians, internists and specialists in general medicine to take a course in family medicine organised by a future institute for family medicine or post-graduate medical training. This obligation should be met by all the doctors mentioned providing basic healthcare after 1 January 2025. The scope of the course will be defined in the framework programme drawn up by the Minister for Health. The amendment provides for the entry into force of the provisions on the organisation of courses in the field of family medicine on 1 January 2019 in order to ensure a 6-year course period. In addition, the aim of the self-correction is to enable the implementation of comprehensive activities of the POZ for disease prevention, including health promotion, education and the identification of risk factors, in local communities, among others. in cooperation with public administrations, including local authorities. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Please continue to proceed with this bill. [[Applause]] [[The opposition applauds because they forgot yours.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u142	Thank you very much. I would like to ask Mrs Margaret Zwierzan to speak on behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u143	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government draft law on basic health care. The purpose of this Act is to provide patients with comprehensive basic healthcare at a high level and to create conditions that will motivate providers to develop and improve the quality of the benefits provided. It should be stressed here that basic health care is the most important in the health system, because it is the point of entry of the patient into the whole system - it is in the POZ that early diagnosis and prevention are provided, that the doctor can properly direct the patient to other levels of health care on the basis of the tests carried out. I must express my appreciation for the bill submitted. I am pleased that the government has noted the need to change the conditions of the systems relating to the role of POZ in the health system. This is the answer to demographic change in our country, in which seniors will arrive every year. Therefore, the introduction of coordinated healthcare is so important. A coordinated model of care will focus on preventive action, which will affect a higher level of health in later stages of life. The proposed bill contains provisions specifying the separate competences of the POZ staff: doctor, nurse and midwife. They will cooperate with each other in maintaining their professional independence. The Act modifies the principles of the right to choose a provider providing basic healthcare services. It allows three options: the choice of doctor, nurse and midwife of POZ with the same provider or the choice of doctor, nurse and midwife of POZ with different providers, as well as the choice of providers who form the POZ team. In addition, the Act gives the right to choose the provider free of charge and at the same time allows it to be changed twice during the calendar year. [[That's the way it is. What's the change?]] The reduction of changes in the provider during the year will have a positive impact on the care of the patient in accordance with the provisions of the Act. I consider the cooperation of the POZ doctor with outpatient care doctors to be the basis of the healthcare model. Thanks to such cooperation, the patient will have access to a wider catalogue of diagnostic tests and specialist consultations. The coordinator of the patient according to the bill submitted is the POZ doctor, whose task in cooperation with the nurse and midwife is to ensure continuity of health care. The legal regulations proposed by the designers will improve the organisation and functioning of the POZ. The proposed rules will help to improve the health of our country's society. We proceed with the examination of item 7 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters and some other laws (Print No. 1814). I ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Justice of Mr Michał Wójcik to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u173	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! I allow myself to present the most important information concerning the draft law amending the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters and some other laws. In fact, this is a situation that sometimes happens when it comes to the legal system, although in theory it should not, namely the legal gap which requires legislative intervention. The question is to cover the costs of criminal proceedings in the course of investigations. As a rule, we have an art. 117 of the Code relating to infringement proceedings, which states that the principle is that any expenditure in such cases is temporarily provided by the Treasury. The exception provided for in this provision concerns the costs of the defenders and the proxy, which shall be provided by the party which established them, possibly. It can be said that, in this situation, temporary costs are, in principle, provided by the State Treasury. But sometimes there are doubts. This concerns, in particular, the situation in which the investigation is conducted, and we are dealing with an authority or an institution which is subject to a local government unit, e.g. a municipal guard or a city guard, which is an organizational unit of the municipality and not a State Treasury. It is known that the municipality has its own personality, its own budget. Indeed, we are faced with a situation where there is no legal basis for imposing an obligation on the municipality, as on the State Treasury, to temporarily cover such costs. It can be said that this situation is inconclusive enough that we have carried out a study, in some units we have carried out such a study to see how many cases concerning investigations carried out in this mode, when actually, for example. the witness does not have refunded travel costs, the cost of participating in such an investigation in the event of a call, or a translator, or an accompanying person, or even an expert in certain situations. We know for sure that these cases may not be too much, but in fact they are deprived, they can be deprived of a person, for example. as a witness, the right of receipt for the costs of participating in such an investigation... This was dealt with differently in the local government. Well, some assumed that the guard should separate a budget and cover costs from it, for example. Witness appearances. Others said that the Treasury should actually cover these costs. In fact, there have been curiosities. There can be doubts, because if from the budget of the municipality, the budget of the local government covers costs, it could even lead to a break in the discipline of public finances. So the situation requires intervention. That's the kind of voice we've been getting. We are therefore proposing appropriate solutions. As part of our solution, expenditure incurred in the course of the investigation will be temporarily paid either by the State Treasury or by the competent local government unit, and will then be settled accordingly. Of course, the question is: What is it? As in general terms, the outcome of the case is decisive. If the procedure is terminated, in this case the costs, which were previously flat-rately covered by the local government unit, by the municipality, will in fact not be reimbursed, i.e. the municipality will have to cover them. It will be the same if the guilty person is acquitted. It's the same situation. In the event of a conviction, this person will have to cover expenditure which, on a lump sum or in advance and on a lump sum basis, will have to be paid in advance by the local government unit, i.e. as on a general basis. Of course, at this point, the question arises as to how this lump sum will be determined, what costs it will be. Here I would like to inform you that, at this moment in the Justice Department, we are preparing an appropriate solution, an appropriate regulation of the Minister of Justice, which will be an implementing act for this, I hope, bill, and there we will provide for an appropriate lump sum for the participation of the person concerned in the proceedings, at the stage of the investigation. And that is the whole point of the proposal, which, as a resort of justice, we are presenting, asking the High House to respond positively to this proposal and to fill the legal gap and at the same time to make the rights of the person concerned to pay its costs, e.g. participation in the proceedings as a witness will be implemented. Thank you very much.

On the basis of the applicable rules, it is not possible to clearly identify an entity which should, as a State Treasury, reimburse the costs incurred in the investigation of offences carried out by the municipal or municipal guards. The Guard is an organisational unit of the municipality which has legal personality and therefore its budget is separate from the State Treasury budget. In the proposed arrangements it was assumed that expenditure incurred in the course of the investigation would be temporarily regulated by the State Treasury or the local government unit and then settled according to the liability for the outcome of the proceedings. For the clearance of expenditure in the final judgment, it was assumed that the expenditure incurred by the Treasury or the local government unit would be flat-rate, except that the lump sum would not cover the costs of appointment of experts or institutions designated to issue opinions on the case and the costs of unpaid legal assistance granted ex officio by lawyers or legal advisers. In the event of termination of proceedings or acquittal, the costs of proceedings will be borne by the State Treasury or by the local government unit, while flat-rate expenses and other charges to the State Treasury or the local government unit will be decided on by the sentenced person. The draft law proposed by the government was quite widely consulted both with the legal environment and, importantly, with local authorities. A large proportion of the comments submitted were taken into account. I would like to draw attention to the proposals which I believe should be considered. For example, according to judges in the area of the Szczecin appeal, the draft law should amend the procedure for challenging the decisions of the investigating authorities. In view of the fact that judgments on the costs of criminal proceedings generally concern small amounts, it would be necessary to consider whether appeal proceedings should be simplified in this respect. In the view of the judges, it would be sufficient to challenge a decision to a regional court in which the seat of the issuing authority of the contested decision is situated. The Ombudsman, in his letter, asked for consideration to be given to a broader justification for the need for an administrative procedure on the costs incurred by a local authority unit on the basis of the proposed Article. 206 § 4 or possibly opting out of this arrangement and maintaining the general rules for the collection of these costs. I would ask the Minister to comment on these two matters. I would like to say, on the other hand, that we are in favour of directing this project to further parliamentary work. He actually fills the legal gap. This issue should therefore be addressed. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u185	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters and certain other laws. In accordance with the justification for the project submitted, the applicant has set itself the objective of addressing the problem of covering the costs of criminal proceedings in the course of investigations carried out by an authority under the authority of a local authority. Above all, we are talking about the explanatory activities carried out by the municipal guard or city guard. Those expenditure should be temporarily covered by the municipalities to which the municipal or municipal guards are subject as their organisational unit under Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 of the 1997 law on municipal guards. However, the current wording of the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters does not provide a legal basis for imposing such an obligation on municipalities. Similarly, it is a matter of indicating the entity responsible for the reimbursement of costs incurred in the investigation of offences carried out by the municipal guard. There is a legal gap which the applicant has rightly decided to remove. The proposed amendment to the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters therefore applies in principle only to Section XIII, i.e. costs of proceedings. However, this is a very important change because the responsible state authorities must ensure that the costs of administrative, civil, criminal or, as in this case, criminal proceedings are regulated in a precise way, which does not generate unduly burdensome expenses for the parties and for the Treasury. The proposals, which in this project in particular need to be underlined, are, first, the provision that expenditure incurred in the course of investigations carried out by an authority under the authority of a local authority unit, i.e. the said municipal guard or municipal guard, will be temporarily provided by the competent local authority unit and will then be settled under the rules governing reimbursement of the costs of proceedings. Secondly, expenditure provided by the State Treasury or local government unit as a result of the proposed amendment will be of a flat-rate nature, with few exceptions, e.g. experts' fees. Finally, thirdly, if convicted, flat-rate expenses and other charges to the Treasury or local government units will be settled, depending on who was the public prosecutor in the case in question, and in the event of the withdrawal of the proceedings or the acquittal of the accused, the costs of the proceedings will be borne by the State Treasury or the local government unit respectively. The bill submitted is a short, but very necessary and expected amendment to the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle will support this bill and request that it be directed to further legislative work in the Committee on Justice and Human Rights. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u197	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! I'm not hiding that... [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Małgorzata Kidawa-Błońska Sejm]] Hello, Your Honor Marshal. Ladies and gentlemen, I do not hide that with great satisfaction today I allow myself to present a draft law on the performance of certain activities of the central authority in family matters in the field of legal trade under European Union law and international agreements. The title of this bill is somewhat complicated and sometimes misleading. Actually, it's about the following thing. There has been no law for 27 years that regulates matters relating to the handling of matters that are carried out under the Hague Convention. In fact, such cases were conducted in a customary manner. It's normal. When the request was received under the Hague Convention, the department concerned was normally dealing with such matters. These are most often parental abduction cases that are conducted under the Hague Convention, but we also have matters of a caring nature and maintenance nature, i.e. three such parts. And in 27 years, unfortunately, no solution has been developed to determine exactly what happens after which, what are the next stages. This would be a guarantee for those who participate in the whole proceedings, but it would also be a guarantee protecting the central body, which in fact is the Ministry of Justice in Poland, which deals with such matters. The second thing that concerns this project is the case of parental abductions under the Hague Convention. So we are most often dealing with a situation in which on the one hand is a citizen of Poland, on the other hand a citizen of another country and a child appears, his well-being. This is usually the case, of course it does not always have to be so, because it is a matter between Polish citizens, and it is obvious. We have decided as a resort of justice to make such a proposal to the High House, which affects a number of issues which are extremely important from the point of view of guarantees for Polish citizens, but above all the children's well-being in the background, because - and here I want to say it very strongly - not always what the Polish citizen says lies in the truth. It is not always that if a Polish citizen says that a child belongs to him for some reason, it means that he is right. We only look in terms of the child's welfare. It's just that. However, it is a fact that over the years the whole mechanism of helping the Polish citizen has not been refined. Often there was a situation where our citizens believed - because the facts were - that they had specific rights to the child and would win without any problem with the law, and then it turned out that these cases were extremely complicated. I've said that a few times. Recently, during the course of the current questions, I responded to the question made by your fellow Members about these very issues, why they are so difficult. I say, because there are criminal elements, civil elements and international elements. So it can be said that the Polish citizen, who defends himself in such a case, wants the child to remain with him, suddenly collids with the machine of defenders on the other hand, with a machine that he is unable to win with, because he will not win the rules. And that was also the reason for us to make appropriate changes to this regulation, which is very important. Of course, there was also a question about the role of the Attorney General, the Ombudsman and the Ombudsman. These are abductive cases that the Ministry of Justice and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and the Ombudsman of the Rights of the Child are concerned about, and so is the various institutions. But actually it was to take this initiative, because it's been going on in parliament for many years. For many years, we have wondered how to deal with this problem and how to avoid tragedy. I can say that these cases, as far as quantity is concerned, maybe there is not a very large number, but as far as specific cases are concerned, they are very difficult. Usually they are extremely delicate and these are cases where there are often dramas - dramas in families, dramas about children. The media is interested in these matters, they are very media, and really things that should be going on quickly, sometimes even within a few weeks - these are the rules - they take place for years. There is a fierce fight between parents about who has rights to a child, a fierce fight that takes place in different countries, before different courts, where there are different proceedings in the background, not only those conducted under the Hague Convention. This is very important. We touch four legal acts: the Code of Civil Procedure, the Police Act, the Law on Family Support and the System of Replacement Care and the Act on Opinional Teams of Experts. Today I want to say very clearly that we are creating something that is not in Poland, and this is a revolution in this area. And I am counting on it, and please all clubs and circles operating in the Polish Parliament, to approach it in an extremely responsible way, so that there is no politics in all of this, and that we are guided by the fact that there is a child's welfare and there is a problem that we have not been able to cope with for many years. What we represent is, in my opinion, a small revolution. We have checked the systems that operate in other countries, and we have tried to choose the best solutions that work in the best systems to put all of this into a single bill, a single draft, and present it to the High House. The first point that is relevant as regards the objective is to organise all the powers and responsibilities of the central authority, the Ministry of Justice. I would like to tell you that, until recently, the Ministry of Justice has dealt with these matters - this is unbelievable - two people. Then these people came. Remember how many Poles left our country. There are 800,000 in Germany alone. people who have only Polish citizenship and double - 1,5 Million. In the UK - almost 1 million. These are huge numbers. You could say there's a problem, because we don't know all the things, we don't need to know, because that's the system. If we get information, of course we react, we try, but until recently, as I said, two people did. Today, the department that has 17 people is dealing with this, but perhaps there will be a department, as is the case in other central bodies across Europe, where we deal with these issues, although it must be made clear: these matters can be dealt with in ministries in individual countries or in a body that is outside the ministry, and that is true too. This is the case in some countries, e.g. in our neighbour, Slovakia, as I recall, this is how it is solved. This is the first thing, which is the law concerning the Ministry of Justice. We'll know every step of the case. The second issue concerns the possibility for the Minister of Justice to exempt citizens from the costs in charge of applications. This matter was raised during the legislative process, when we created it during the discussions that we held on the Permanent Committee of the Council of Ministers, on the Council of Ministers. How's this gonna work? It's a general rule. The Minister of Justice will decide whether or not a person can be exempted from these costs, taking into account different solutions, different elements, different circumstances. The third point is to simplify the forms of communication and proposals in order to ensure the speed of identification of cases. These things can't go on for years. Here the first and basic form is e-mail, a form that uses IT tools, and then only a traditional form. Speed is a very important issue. Deformity, speed of expert recognition. The point is that opinions in these cases should be issued much earlier than in other cases. This is very important. Another case is the mandate of the Minister of Justice to obtain information on the whereabouts of the child also through the Police. Here was the matter of the police, her involvement in all of this. It's about searching, it's about how. Does that mean the police can pick up the baby? No. We will explain if, of course, there are such questions that there are strict rules on how a child can be picked up. They must be strictly respected. Another issue concerns a certain institutionalisation in this area. With regard to the courts of first instance, these cases were dealt with by regional courts, they still deal with them, and in second instance, by district courts. It is three hundred and dozen courts that deal with cases that are extremely difficult. A few thousand judges. Now, state your role as judge to whom such a case is brought. It is to read the Hague Conventions, various, Brussels II bis regulation, read various international regulations. He's supposed to know things in a criminal or civil context. Those are problems. We don't think that was a good solution. Three hundred and dozen courts are not a good solution. We're a specialist judiciary. There will be 11 courts, not district courts, only district courts, one for each area of jurisdiction of the court of appeal. One at a time. As far as appeals are concerned, there have been 45 such district courts so far. We're introducing a rule that there's gonna be a single court of appeal. It will be a specially created department at the Court of Appeal in Warsaw. What does that mean, ladies and gentlemen? That means we're creating a specialized judiciary. And I would like to say to you that we are one of the few countries of the European Union that has no specialised judiciary. There's no such court. It's been a big problem so far. We are, of course, walking here in line with the EU's solutions. It's a shame it hasn't been done in years. There's gonna be specialist law enforcement. What does that mean? This means that 30, maybe 40 people in Poland will deal with such cases, I mean judges, not a few thousand. What does that give us? It's simple, that's where the professionals will go, that's where the people who will be trained will go. I have already spoken to the director of the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office, where, I hope, there will be training with the best lecturers, so that these people will be provided with the best possible knowledge to have information on how such matters should be resolved. 40 people, not a few thousand. This is important. In a court of appeal, the several-member department, that's also important, and not a lot of judges who deal with these kinds of cases. That's not all. There will be, ladies and gentlemen, an emergency appeal that will be served by the Attorney General. It's about the cassation complaint. There are situations, and that's an undeniable fact, when the ministry comes to us, cases where, at first glance, you can see that something is wrong. What can the Minister of Justice do in such a case? All he can do is ask for custody of the case, ask for some explanations. We get all kinds of explanations, ladies and gentlemen, which are sometimes amazing. It's not that I care about judges anymore, because there are great judges, that's what I think, it's obvious, but there are people who shouldn't take care of these things. Unfortunately, this does not bring us pride as a state, that after a judgment at first instance a child can leave our country, be transported to another country, and then the matter becomes really more complicated. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u199	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The Minister has touched everyone in a unique, special, very exhaustive way, and at the same time the most important, because it is such a sensitive issue, matters which concern the material aspects of the bill. I will allow myself, fully supporting this project on behalf of our club, to address the formal aspects, because it is equally important. The primary objective of the government's draft law on the execution of certain activities of the central authority in family matters in the field of legal trade under European Union law and international agreements is to define the principles and procedures of the Polish central authority, Polish courts and other Polish authorities in matters covered by four acts of international and Community law. The acts in question are listed in Article. 1 project. This applies to, among others. the European Convention on the Recognition and Enforcement of Decisions relating to the custody of a child and the Restoration of Child Care, which was drawn up in Luxembourg, the Convention on the Civil Aspects of the Abduction of a Child Abroad, namely the Hague Convention, the Convention on Jurisdiction, applicable law, recognition, enforcement and cooperation in matters of parental responsibility and measures to protect children. These conventions as international agreements form part of the national legal order and are directly applicable, which results inter alia. from the Polish Constitution. On the other hand, the Regulation is of general scope and directly binds the Member States of the European Union, in accordance with the Treaty on the Functioning of the Union. Those acts shall specify the tasks of the central authorities of standards which may be applied directly without being supplemented by national rules. It should be noted, however, that the same acts also contain standards which regulate certain spheres of operation of central authorities in a general and non-specific manner, so that they can only be applied in conjunction with national standards. In matters of parental responsibility with a cross-border dimension, cooperation between the various authorities of the countries concerned is essential and is significantly impeded without any clarification of the specific rules. In many situations, the Polish central body operates on the basis of the Polish law applied by analogy, which was created in isolation from the acts mentioned, often many years before their creation, and subsequent amendments did not lead to the regulation of this issue. Using only the interpretation of the law in force now raises many problems, and this may raise doubts. on the validity of certain actions taken by the Polish Central Authority on the basis of these acts of law and does not encourage the broadcasting of matters quickly, despite their exceptional nature - they concern children. The proposed law seeks to regularise issues that are not regulated by law or are not sufficiently detailed. To some extent, the project also includes a way of dealing with the Polish Central Authority, leading to improvement of its work. The draft law notes that the Polish Central Authority designated for the purposes of the Article. 1 of the conventions and regulations will, of course, be the Minister of Justice. The period of 6 months adopted in the proposed act should be sufficient time to prepare the entities covered by this project to carry out the tasks assigned to them. So much for the formal aspect. What the Minister has said is clearly a substantive aspect which should translate into these formal matters, and from a legal point of view they are also essential. While we are aware of the need for legal changes in this area, the proposals presented in the government project are positive. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Lawyer's coercion is also to be introduced. Where the parent cannot afford a lawyer, the representative shall be appointed by office. The next changes are the introduction of the possibility of an appeal before the Supreme Court. An important change is to introduce the principle that until the appeal hearing, the child will not be able to be delivered abroad. So far, decisions concerning the return of children have been executed at the time of their announcement at first instance, although the Hague Convention did not require this. This change too, Minister, deserves recognition. The project also sets out the modalities for obtaining data on the potential foster family and the rules for transferring children to foster care abroad as part of the revision of the Civil Procedure Code and the Law on supporting the family and foster care system. The Advocate General, the Ombudsman and the Ombudsman of the rights of the child shall be given the opportunity to bring a cassation action before the Supreme Court in cases of unlawful abduction or detention of a person under parental or guardianship. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u221	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position in the following point: First reading of the government draft law on the performance of certain activities of the central authority in the field of family legal trade under European Union law and international agreements, print No 1827. The purpose of this law is to define the powers and responsibilities of the Polish Central Authority in matters of a very sensitive nature, covered by international documents, i.e.: the 1980 European Convention of Luxembourg, the 1980 Hague Convention, the 1996 Hague Convention and Council Regulation No 2201/2003. They are all dealt with at international and EU level and relate to a set of rules governing the issuing, recognition and enforcement of decisions taken in international matters of child abduction or detention. It is a necessary regulation, which was clearly lacking in the Polish legal system. Poland, together with many other European countries, creates an international system of fighting for the welfare of children. It would seem that the signing of conventions, which thus become Polish law, is sufficient. However, this is not the case. There is always a need to adapt Polish rules, procedures to the requirements of a given convention. And so it is in this case. For many years Poland has fulfilled its international obligations resulting, among others. from the Hague Convention. However, we know that everything could look better, function more efficiently, and thus save the trauma and bad experiences of hundreds of children who each year become victims of immature adults. This could be the case if, like other countries, we had already defined the central authority and adapted the Polish law, thus giving space and support to international law. This project, establishing binding procedures and adapting inter alia. Civil proceedings to conventional requirements are the way in the right direction. The Act lays down in a definitive manner that the Polish central authority coordinating the activities of the Convention is the Minister of Justice. The procedure for dealing with matters dealt with by the central authority is also regulated, although to a narrow extent. The length of proceedings is reduced, the number of judges is increasing in the general courts to deal with these cases. Additional financial resources are foreseen. The planned changes in Polish k.p.c. provide for a hearing of the 1980 Hague Convention cases concerning the unlawful abduction or detention of a child to elected district courts and one appeal court, which will allow the specialisation of judges and the concentration of the availability of translators and professional representatives. Similar solutions can be observed in other countries Parties to the Hague Convention. The Act obliges many state bodies, including the Minister of Justice, the Ombudsman, the Ombudsman, the General Courts, the Police, the curators, or the local authorities. Most of them took part in the consultation and opinion of the bill. Not all comments were accepted. Perhaps over some it is worth to bend over in the course of further work. It would be good practice to create further such laws. Many conventions and provisions of EU law are waiting for some form of implementation. What about the UN Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, ratified by Poland on 6 September 2012? Although it was signed by your government on 20 March 2007, it has not been properly implemented to date. For this, a little good will is enough, and thousands of people with disabilities would have greater comfort in life. The State is obliged to take care of its citizens and to create decent living conditions for them, especially those vulnerable, children, people excluded due to age, disability or derogatory living conditions. As many examples show, it is not the art of creating a good law or signing it, it is important to be able to implement it and respect it - every day, in the smallest situations. May the law discussed today be the best example of this.
