We proceed with the examination of item 9 of the agenda: Report from the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Health on the Government's draft law amending the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare and the Act on Liability for Violation of Public Finance Discipline (Prints No. 1791 and 1844). I ask Mr Krzysztof Szulowski to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u223	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report of the Joint Committees: the Committee on Health and Public Finance on the Government's draft law amending the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare and the Act on Liability for Violation of Public Finance Discipline, Prints 1791 and 1844. The project has four main objectives. The first is the creation of a legal framework for pilot schemes to check planned new systemic arrangements for the organisation, implementation or financing of health care services. There has been no statutory regulation so far and therefore testing new ways of organising healthcare has been difficult. Pilot programmes will allow for derogations from existing rules and conditions for the provision of healthcare services in order to verify that the planned changes in the organisation, implementation and financing of healthcare services will contribute to improving the situation of patients and will be effective from the point of view of the health system. To this end, the Act defines the concept of a pilot scheme as a team of health care activities aimed at testing the implementation of new arrangements for the organisation, implementation or financing of healthcare services and identifies what will define the scope of such a programme. The Minister for Health and the President of the National Health Fund has been authorised to draw up and establish a pilot programme by Regulation and Order respectively. The Minister responsible for Health will be empowered to supervise the pilot programmes established by the Minister for Health and Control, while the National Health Fund will be competent to implement, finance, monitor and evaluate pilot programmes. The possibility of financing pilot programmes from the State budget is also introduced, from the part of which the Minister responsible for Health is the responsibility. Monitoring their effectiveness and evaluation are essential for the effectiveness of pilot programmes. To this end, the pilot programme will need to identify indicators for the implementation of the pilot programme, how to measure implementation indicators and how to assess the results of the pilot programme, in order to assess what possible changes should be made to the organisation of the medicinal processes or the way in which the funding is financed. The second objective of the draft law is to simplify and clarify the procedure for the development of the health policy programme and to ensure standardisation of the actions undertaken. According to the proposed solutions, the draft health policy programme should be developed not only on the basis of data derived from health needs maps but also on the basis of epidemiological data available. At the same time, the project introduces new solutions to develop a health policy programme on the basis of a recommendation from the President of the Agency for the Evaluation of Medical Technologies and Tarification issued for a given disease or a given health problem, which has been prepared on the basis of scientific evidence. The draft provides that the President of the Agency will issue opinions only for draft health policy programmes for which appropriate recommendations have not been published. Recommendations will be drawn up after prior verification of the Agency's assumptions of the draft health policy programmes on the basis of which the Agency will prepare a report on the recommended medical technologies, the actions carried out under the health policy programmes and the conditions for implementing those programmes concerning the disease concerned or the health problem concerned. The Transparency Council will then issue opinions on the basis of the Agency's report. Recommendations will be published in the health policy repository, which will be made available in the Agency's Public Information Bulletin and the Agency's website. The draft law sets out the content of the recommendation. From the date of publication of the recommendation of the President of the Agency, the body drawing up the draft health policy programme will be required to take into account the recommendations contained therein in the draft programme. In view of the need to ensure uniform standards for the creation and monitoring of the results of the health policy programme, which has been completed, the draft provides for the implementation of uniform programme models and the final report on the implementation of the programme. It has been established that there will be no need for the Agency to address draft health policy programmes, which are the continuation of programmes that have already been positively assessed. This will apply to the unchanged health policy programme implemented in the previous period for which the Agency's President has given a positive or conditional positive opinion and health policy programmes already implemented but updated where their objectives are not changed. It was introduced as a condition for the start of the implementation, implementation and financing of the health policy programme to obtain a positive or conditional positive opinion of the President of the Agency or to send a statement by the body drawing up the draft programme on its compliance with the recommendation of the President of the Agency. The negative opinion of the President of the Agency on the draft health policy programme will therefore not be able to implement, implement and finance such a health policy programme. Another important change in relation to the current legal situation is the introduction of a solution giving the entity that has developed, implemented, implemented and financed the health policy programme, the possibility of suspending in whole or in part the implementation and financing of the programme in the event of a lack of funding or of reducing the financial resources for its implementation. Where the implementation of the programme is not resumed for a period of 2 years, it shall be deemed to have been completed and the entity implementing the programme shall be required to submit, on the basis of general principles, a final report on the implementation of the programme. The entity implementing and financing the health policy programme will be required to complete its implementation in the event of circumstances indicating that it is not intended to continue to be implemented. This may be the case, for example. in the event of a change in the recommendation of the President of the Agency or the revision of the scientific guidelines for the intervention concerned or the emergence of new scientific reports on the disease or the health problem concerned, as well as in the event of an earlier achievement of the programme's objectives. The next objective of the draft law is to enable the Minister responsible for health access to personal data contained in the electronic health programme monitoring system, currently under the name of the Prevention Monitoring IT System, and to amend the procedure for selecting a health policy programme implemented by the Minister responsible for Health. The possibility for the National Health Fund to collect and process data obtained from the implementation of health programmes and to monitor their quality is clarified. It shall be possible for the Minister responsible for Health to entrust the implementation of the health policy programme without the need to conduct a competition for tenders, which shall be acceptable in situations relating to the inability to conduct a competition for tenders within the prescribed time limit and in the case of a limited number of potential developers. The National Health Fund will be able to provide funding for health policy programmes implemented by local government units for the provision of health benefits as defined in the lists of guaranteed benefits and not, as currently, for the financing of non-guaranteeed benefits programmes. Finally, the fourth objective of the Act is to abolish the restriction on access to treatment for groups of patients suffering from disorders burdened with a negative social assessment, guaranteed benefits in addiction treatment facilities - family members and people close to addicts, pathological gamblers or persons with other disorders of habits and urges. A total of 13 amendments were tabled by two committees, the Health Commission and the Public Finance Commission yesterday during the procedure for the bill and were all accepted. The entire draft law was given a positive opinion by a majority and the committees recommend that the High House adopt it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u224	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u265	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! As a representative of the applicants, I am pleased to present to you the parliamentary bill on defence and ammunition, printing No. 1692, together with auto-corrections taking into account BAS's comments, which is the result of the work of a wide range of social organizations related to shooting, sports shooting and such organizations that bring together lovers and gun collectors. This law lays down rules on the possession and acquisition of arms and ammunition in the territory of the Republic of Poland, the disposal, storage, carrying, handling, use, transport and export of weapons and the operation of shooting galleries. The proposed law on arms and ammunition is intended to replace the Law of 21 May 1999 on arms and ammunition. This amendment is primarily dictated by the need to achieve full harmonisation of the Polish legal order concerning the control of the acquisition and possession of firearms with the rules in force within the European Union, such as the revised Directive 2008/51 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 21 May 2008 amending the previous Directive, with the latest amendment of the EU Directive, which our law absolutely does not comply with and is practically not suitable for such legislative improvement as would allow these elements to be fulfilled. According to the Constitution, Poland is a democratic state of law, which means more or less that the legalism of public authorities and officials should be one of the pillars of its statehood. However, despite the different changes and the good direction of these changes, there are still areas where, unfortunately, the former system has been known to have arbitrary discretion in administrative decisions. And this is the area where, for example, the legal regulations on access to arms are in force. And it is many people in Poland who feel it. The current law on arms and ammunition, although adopted in 1999, unfortunately continues a little bit of this thought from the former system in its approach to arms, which is in fact based on the treatment of access to arms as a certain attribute of power and the privilege of its favorable persons and of refusing access to it to persons fulfilling basically the requirements of the law, and who are ordinary citizens. It must be acknowledged here that the rules in force in the European Union have forced certain modifications, to a certain, yet insufficient degree, Europeanising the Polish legal state. This special treatment of the legal possession of firearms by citizens to a certain extent is a tribute to the fact that in some police circles there is still a way of thinking appropriate to the previous system, according to which the police, as if to represent this armed arm of the state, are not fully fulfilling the obligation which is based on helping citizens, but rather some place to stand up against these citizens. And although most of the staff has already been exchanged for natural reasons, we often deal with such elements rather than other thinking. The amendment of the law on arms and ammunition is also part of the process of rebuilding a strong, sovereign Polish state. The lost independence of Poland in 1939 will actually be regained when the Polish state authority gives Poles the right to own weapons which were taken away from them by German and Russian invaders. Czechs, Lithuanians, Slovaks, Hungarians and other nations that came out of the same post-Soviet boiler can long have weapons on normal European principles, Poles still can't. We've still got a government that's about as large as 1 000 gun permits. residents than the average in the countries of the European Union. In Poland these are approximately 3 licences, the average of the European Union - approx. 30. It actually shows the scale of the difference. This limits our country's defence capabilities and blocks the development of Polish industry. Actually, today we have only two small arms factories and two small arms factories, all with very modest manufacturing capabilities. In comparison, the Czechs are practically dominant in this market when it comes to the countries of Central Europe. They dominated the arms market and the ammunition market. The level of crime in Poland is roughly equal to the average level in the countries of the European Union, i.e. disarmament does not actually raise the level of security in our country in any way. In this context, it is very important to create a large market segment in the economy of the country with significant annual revenues for the state budget. And that's what the shareholders are talking about, the presidents of weapons companies in Poland. In addition to the arms sales network, this segment of the economy also consists of shooters with instructors, training facilities, gunsmith workshops, private museums and, most importantly, clubs bringing together people of different interests, which foster the development of civil society. In addition, in the age of the professional army, at the moment also the territorial defence of the country is beyond overestimating the situation in which individuals want to improve themselves, they want to train in shooting sports, in the use of weapons. Those who want to learn this with their own money and with their own weapons are very valuable to every country. In the hour of danger, they'll be the weight of gold. At the moment, Poland has a unique period of serenity at the borders in its history, when no one of its neighbours is in fact threatening it in an important way, its statehood, i.e. it does not bring significant claims, but this state cannot last forever and today we should also be aware of it. Let us remember that always at the moment when they wanted to conquer Poland in any way, enslave or addict Poland, they sought to disarm Poles. Unfortunately, today we continue such a sad Peerelow tradition of disarmed Poland. Access to weapons in Poland is still treated as an attribute of a certain privilege, a certain reward, a little maybe even an attribute of power, as a guarded privilege of power belonging primarily to persons associated with certain structures, as if situated above in the social hierarchy. The other citizens can only aspire to this privilege by means of an organization of a monopolistic character, but also very specific in terms of formation: whether it be the Polish Strzelecki Union or the Polish Łowiecki Union. You really don't have to be a hunter today to have a gun, learn how to shoot or use a gun in a shooting range. These are two different things, so we should also pay attention here. But the essence is what this law introduces and what it concerns, and it also applies to a very large number of important issues. Although it is not marked as a law relating to the regulation of Union law, it nevertheless concerns EU law and its failure to introduce or at all not to amend the law on arms and ammunition will also have some consequences on this canvas. The most important of the proposed changes to the current legislation that this law brings... There is a lot of talk about this bill, everyone talks about it, and almost nobody in Poland has read it. I read newspapers, I listen to journalists, and I don't really know what bill they're writing about and talking about, because it's certainly not about the one we put in here and presented, so it's really worth remembering a few things. Firstly, it is a change in the concept of arms permits issued for the declared purpose of use. There is an amendment to permits for a specific set of technical weapons, as is the case with driving licences issued, for example, for passenger cars up to 3.5 tonnes of total weight, and not for example. for the purpose of travelling on holiday or for the purpose of commuting to work. Such stratification is in line with the European Directive and the practice in most countries of the European Union. Secondly, the real departure from discretion in the issue of arms permits. The law clearly sets out the strict positive conditions to be met for the issue of a permit for weapons and the strict negative conditions to prevent the authorisation from being granted. The licensing authority is simply to apply the relevant provisions, as is the case for the issue of licences, licences or similar. To date, arbitrary discretion in the issue of arms permits has not only hindered access to arms for ordinary citizens, but also unacceptable cases of the issue of legal permits for professional weapons to criminals, for example. various police confidants. We have also dealt with this, and anyone who has ever applied for a weapon, also knows what the road through torment is and how, at the end of the day, meeting even the requirements of the current law, you can not get this weapon. 'Cause at the end of the seat, you're gonna say, "You didn't convince me." Thirdly, the introduction of an effective system for monitoring the health of the gun-holder, in particular his mental health, resulting in the immediate withdrawal of the permit in the event of a disease which excludes the possibility of carrying a weapon. The proposed bill sets out these diseases precisely, while at the same time eliminating the currently functioning discriminatory records of persons with disabilities, for example, the organ of movement or hearing, which, in our opinion, has nothing to do with restricting the right to own arms. The current system of medical examinations appears to be aimed primarily at hindering access to a weapon's permit rather than real system security. In our project actually at every moment, every day when a family doctor will be able to determine that a person has been sick with a given disease, he shows certain signs of illness which, according to the law, prevent him from carrying a weapon, he is obliged to report it, and there is such a reaction to the fact that the weapon is taken away. Fourthly, the introduction of objective rules to check the competence of an aspiring person to carry a weapon when justified, i.e. primarily as regards the wearing of weapons in hiding in public places. Today it does not exist, there is no such control every certain time, it is issued once and later not checked. This is served by a special competence test, periodically repeated, checking for elementary shooting skills in the use of weapons under stress. In this way, any person wishing to gain the right to carry a weapon to self-defense would have to prove that he had a certain level of skill, but he would not have to prove a greater threat than he did today. Ladies and gentlemen, weapons are a deterrent element, they're a preventative element, so it can't really be that I have to get mugged first to prove that I need weapons. This weapon is basically part of defense, already passive defense, which means I won't be assaulted because I may have it, and the criminal also counts. It should be noted that the current general rules do not assume that this type of competence should be checked, as the right to bear arms is now a privilege granted on the basis of discretionaryly assessed imponderabilies. Fifthly, the introduction of effective mechanisms for tracking the history of weapons in the form of a uniform electronic system of recording of weapons permits, copies of weapons and all purchase-sales transactions. What's important is that it's uniform across the country. Currently, one separate registration system is used by the police and the other is completely incompatible with the police, by the Military Police. This is a far-reaching solution, because in fact such registers - fully functioning and efficient - do not yet have European countries. Some partial solutions are available, for example, in Germany, but are also only at the stage of refinement of these elements. And here we could be the leader again in this matter actually. Sixthly, a change in the authority issuing the gun permit. We propose that, like the Second Republic of Poland, a permit be given to the governor or the president of the city accordingly. Currently, police commanders are authorised by police officers under the service command of the Chief of Police, which is not the body mentioned in the Act, but through dependence, the service subsidiary has the right to shape the policy of issuing arms permits. In fact, the rules there are formulated by a number of people employed in the Police Headquarters, which is worse, they can change by 180 degrees as a result of momentary emotions, and in the changed letter of the law. Other rules are in force in the Military Gendarmery, which is the issuing authority for the licensing of weapons to professional soldiers, which also introduces a state of inconsistencies in the application of the law. And that's the subject. The river, if we were to tell you how it works right now in the police force. And I am absolutely in favour of the police no longer being a judge on their own behalf. What is the level of the administrative authority, we can discuss in committee, and it should not be police authorities at all. This system just didn't work out and it doesn't make sense to go into something that doesn't work or works very poorly, and people feel it very often on their own, as if recently when using a gun with rubber balls, where they actually bought it in good faith in licensed stores, and then the police appeared and there were legal problems. Seventhly, the precise definition of the substance concepts of weapons and ammunition used in the Act is to eliminate once and for all the interpretation doubts which are an immanent feature of the current legislation. The current law on arms and ammunition has lost consistency after numerous amendments and therefore no longer fulfils the function of an effective regulator sensitive to the security of a fragment of reality. As an illustration of this incoherence, let us use the first example on the shore relating to weapons used for hunting. Art. 4 of the present law mentions hunting weapons, art. 10 - about weapons for hunting purposes, but the interrelationship of these terms has not been determined in any way. The source of this dualism is one of the little carefully implemented amendments to the law, and we have a problem with that too. This law introduces a clear vocabulary, clearly defined concepts. Finally, we are done with this interpretation, which unfortunately we all had to deal with. After the eighth, implementing in the Polish legal order the amendments introduced by the revision of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on control of the acquisition and possession of weapons of 17 May 2017, i.e. the latest amendment. This Directive has introduced significant changes to the rules on access to certain categories of weapons, in particular certain types of weapons included in the extended category A, as well as the inclusion of museums and private collectors in the Directive. So far, both museums and collectors have not been included in the scope of the directive. The scope of the individual categories A, B and C has changed and category D has been eliminated by transferring the weapons contained in it to category C. A regular monitoring of the state of compliance of the holder of the arms permit with the conditions for its issue, in particular the state of health, has been introduced at intervals not exceeding 5 years, with alternative continuous monitoring being permitted. Several definitions and rules for the carriage of weapons have also been amended on the basis of the European Firearms Card across the intra-EU borders. These changes have led, as I have already mentioned, to a significant loss of compliance with the EU Directive. The proposed proposal for a law on arms and ammunition implements all these changes, often more stringent than the minimum required by the directive, e.g. weapons belonging to category C, i.e. requiring only registration, in accordance with the draft law, may be held on the basis of a licence from Mon. Citizen's gun card, so we'll go a little further. In place of five-year verification periods, the draft law proposes a system of continuous monitoring, resulting in automatic withdrawal of the permit for weapons in the event of negative conditions, i.e. convictions for a specific offence or diagnosis of a particular medical condition, I mentioned this earlier. The project introduces permits for museums - collectors have long been subject to the arms licensing regime in Poland - and lays down the principles of carrying weapons across the borders of the European Union in line with the revised directive. The proposal also introduces EU-compliant definitions of the terms used in the Act, including e.g. alarm weapon, currently identical with gas weapon, which is absolutely incompatible with the professional nomenclature. Previously, the rules distinguished them, without defining them clearly enough, which was the reason for the problems of interpretation. The draft law also introduces a stratification of the weapons category precisely in line with the new directive, including effectively recognises the very difficult implementation of the issue of large-capacity replaceable magazines - for short-armed weapons, respectively, 20 rounds, for long-armed weapons of 10 rounds. These magazines do not constitute a substantial part of the weapons nor do they require permission to possess them, but the possibility of their purchase and use has now been limited by the Directive to persons who have been permitted to use them in category A7. To sum up, the proposed proposal for a law on arms and ammunition is a modern act prepared in accordance with the best legislative principles and with the utmost care both in substance and in law and in accordance with the European Union legislation governing access to arms. It was developed in a legalistic licensing spirit in place of the current opportunistic licensing legislation. This project is also groundbreaking in the sense that it radically breaks up with the legacy of the previous era. The present law is de facto written on the basis of the communist law of 1961, that is, anachronism, and duplicates its conceptual model, which, on the one hand, made it as difficult as possible for a person without connections to obtain a weapon, on the other hand, treated people belonging to a group of privileged persons very liberally. This break-up will destroy the current arrangement of interest groups and enslaved coteries in the procedure of separating the privilege of access to weapons, leading to the elimination of the related pathology. The principles of access to arms will be the same for all citizens, objective and rational. At the same time, the regulatory mechanisms provided for in the draft law, including automated procedures for withdrawal of permits in situations where there are grounds for excluding the possibility of carrying a weapon, will ensure an increase in the level of public security in this respect, although the number of arms holders will probably increase after the implementation of this draft law, for example because no one will be discriminated against, i.e. those who comply with the statutory conditions, will receive this permit. Today we are not dealing with this, and here is the fundamental problem. If we achieve through this law, thanks in fact to the fact that we will abide by the provisions of the law, the average European level when it comes to the possession of weapons by citizens, it will still be very successful. I think this is the upper limit of what we can count on under this law. Dear Messengers, Dear Members, I would ask you to direct this project to further work on the committee. I think that we can disagree in detail, we can talk about it, but we absolutely should not at the moment decide to block a project which, first, meets the requirements that the Union imposes on us, and in a moment we will be asked to meet these requirements, secondly, a project that really introduces modern legislation in Poland. It's not really clear, a project that gives people guns. This is a project that organizes the legal system in Poland and clearly defines the rules who, why and when can receive this weapon. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

We cannot make such drastic changes without proper preparation, because it would be extremely irresponsible and the consequences of this - difficult to predict. Article 11 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 that the gun permit will be issued for an indefinite period of time. The person permitted to hold a weapon must be checked for mental predisposition, for example, on a periodic basis, specialist tests should be carried out to verify that there are no changes that may affect the ability to hold a weapon and that knowledge of the applicable rules should be reviewed, all in order to ensure safety. The Convention also provides for other international organisations which, through their regulations and their bodies, also monitor these issues, inter alia. The Council of Europe and its Parliamentary Assembly. We're a member of this organization, too. I am also saying this from the point of view of my experience, because for two years - or even three years - I was the chairman of a delegation to the Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe, for two years Vice-President. In the Council of Europe, there is a so-called. CPT Committee, Committee on the Functioning of the European Union prevention of torture, which monitored it, was present in Poland and prepared a report on it. His conclusions include: the lack of implementation of the law in this area, i.e. the absence of a definition of torture and the definition and penalisation of crimes which may result from the actions of persons, I stress this very clearly, related to institutions and public functions which have a bearing on these crimes - crimes which are deliberately linked to the suffering of persons and their humiliation. The definitions of torture also differentiate not only this action, which is of the nature of additional punishment, enforcement of information or testimony in an unlawful manner, but also those activities which are discriminatory in nature or are an authorisation for such activities - and therefore also penallise or recommend that the same level of penalisation as if the person was also involved in this process. I propose that the recommendations resulting from the Convention be included in the Penal Code, in Article. 246 with three paragraphs, which I'll explain. This is precisely the distinction. Thus, these are three paragraphs devoted, as the Convention states, to the criminalisation of both those actions which concern the direct enforcement of testimony, information or statements, as well as actions which are discriminatory and slightly different in nature, but are physically identical. Actions with particular cruelty have been identified as those which should be particularly punishable. But there is also § 4, to which I would like to point out: the practice and criminalisation at the same level and with the same level of penalty of action, which allows for the actions mentioned above, i.e. the participation in this process of persons who supervise, persons who have knowledge, persons who permit such offences. It is extremely important to punish and enforce criminal liability also against those who, in the absence of this definition today, in the absence of precise records in this regard, most often act exactly the opposite: they cover up such crimes, cause them not to come to light, or minimize the significance of these crimes. For from what do I and many people, as well as the institutions that I have mentioned, show that this revision of the Penal Code is necessary? For example, from the image and from the situations we have at the Polish police stations. How appalling these stories are, we have recently had an example - Igor Stachowiak - the one who saw the light of day. I remind you, in May 2016, Igor Stachowiak was murdered in the toilet with a taser. He was tortured by several police officers. Moreover, this case has been covered up for a year. We heard, just like we used to, years ago... It seemed that after 1989, after changing the militia to the police, it would be the security guarantee and this security would be rebuilt in our citizens' feelings, and we would not hear such sentences as once in one of the reports: beat so that there were no traces. It was shown here that the taser was used not only in a deliberate manner, to cause pain, but also in a totally humiliating way, a man who was handcuffed, helpless, asked for help, asked to stop. This beasty thing can't go unpunished. What's our situation today? How often do we have a situation? Because I can talk about many, many cases. I'm about to respond to them. The most commonly used is § 231, i.e. exceeding powers. Most often, the situation is directed towards disciplinary action, and we are talking about the possibility of expulsion from the service. And yet this is a crime that absolutely requires bringing the finals to the court. There must be a true judgment of this act, a double unworthy act, because not only in relation to cruelty, not only related to crime - in this case murder - but committed by an officer, who is to give security guarantees, a public officer, who is to be the one in the face of whom even one who has broken the law, knows that the law will be applied and applied in a fair, adequate way, without exceeding the rules and without impunity precisely. And what's the picture of the police in recent years? This is not just a matter of recent years, I would like to draw attention to it because we are aware of it. I can list many examples from the last few days that we know about. This was highlighted by the Ombudsman Adam Bodnar in his report. The Igor Stachowiak case. That's the death at the police station in Wroclaw. But I also remember perfectly well the images we saw, recorded by telephone, images that caused the protests in Bialystok. The death of a young Jagiellonian fan who was suspected of being drunk was strangled and strangled, even though people asked, said that he was losing his breath, died, that he had to step away from this action. What happened to the officers responsible? Chief of Police Voivodeship in Białystok Daniel Kołnierowicz received a general promotion. Is this really acceptable? Shouldn't this case have a real criminal dimension? Can supervisory and responsible persons be promoted after such an event? Same thing happened a few years ago in Siedlce. I'm guessing three cops tortured three people. The question asked was even more shocking: Do you prefer Guantanamo or Belarus? And what happened to the senator who was talking about his police officers being exemplary police officers? This year he was promoted to Rector of the Police School in Szczytno. What's he gonna teach? What example is to be given, and what example should it be, if such events occurred under his supervision in the district headquarters if there is no paragraph or finale in the form of a real punishment for the perpetrators who did so? Let me remind you that of three people suspected of stealing a little something, one boy is dead. Torture, which is used by public officials, does not concern big cases. These are not actions aimed at particularly cruel and ruthless murderers or criminals. They appear when, on the other hand, the victim becomes very often either a small thief, or a person rolled up somewhere along the way, perhaps in a drunken state, as in a few cases that involved drunk drivers. Reports that concern Poland also include these statistics. They're terrifying. When I was the head of the subcommittee - the Permanent Sub-committee, it was in the previous term - together with the Helsinki Commission and the Ombudsman, we also worked together with the Police to educate and avoid this kind of story without consequences, so that it would not affect and affect the image of the entire Police. I'm talking about this not just to accuse everyone, just to eliminate these black sheep and so that no case reported and described is left without consequences. The problem is that civil oversight, the knowledge of the Ombudsman, as well as the parliamentary committee, which has been set up for this purpose, do not exist in this term, and the BSW itself, which was a partner for us at the time, is now directly subordinate to the minister and in fact has become secret. The documents that concern Poland are fresh documents and at least two, which come from the last year, I wanted to recall. These are the final remarks concerning the 7th interim report of Poland. The Human Rights Committee, the UN-Etonic Agenda, points out in points 25 and 26. - I refer to this document - that, as regards the prohibition of torture and the cruel, inhuman treatment of persons, the Committee is concerned that, despite the signing of declarations and conventions in 2002, despite the government's assurances of implementation of the law, the offence of torture is not considered a criminal offence in the Penal Code, and therefore there is no definition and there is no direct reference beyond the enigmatic article. 247, it is not fully taken into account, and therefore it is also fully validated. The Committee is also concerned that excessive use of force is still being recorded, and that, moreover, it is not just the police, it concerns the Prison Service, it concerns other areas - cases of covering up or ignoring the signals. In point 26., i.e. in the recommendation, there is clear information and conclusion that the State Party to the Convention, i.e. Poland, the Polish Government, should, firstly, amend the Penal Code in this respect in order to ensure that all elements of the crime of torture are prohibited in accordance with Article. 7 of the Pact, and also lay down sanctions for acts of torture commensurate with the gravity of this crime, and thus in line with those definitions I mentioned earlier. It should also be ensured that all allegations and complaints made concerning acts of torture and ill-treatment are promptly and properly investigated. The State Party should also ensure that the alleged perpetrators of those acts are held liable and, in the event of conviction, also punishable in accordance with those judgments. A very important document, currently discussed in the Council of Europe, is the monitoring of the CPT committee, i.e. the one that monitors... which visited Poland in 2016. I would remind you that the European Committee for the Prevention of Torture and Inhuman or Demeaning Treatment or Punishment makes it very clear that non-fulfilment of the law is a violation of the Convention and that the lack of definition means poor education but, above all, a lack of criminalisation, which is already a very strong point of the committee. In this connection, the role of the responsible state and responsible politicians, Mr President, is also such as to shape criminal policy to sanction the phenomena which society considers to be negative, harmful and sometimes even criminal. There is also no need to convince anyone that violence against another person is criminal, and violence with particular cruelty deserves severe punishment, and extremely severe treatment deserves those who, due to their public functions, should protect people from this violence. As Dr. Adam Bodnar said in one of his public speeches, the Ombudsman, thinking about torture only in a historical context is a mistake. Here's the full approval. We must see that the phenomenon of torture is also taking place in Poland in the 21st century. Speaking of torture, we cannot think only of the use of violence by individuals towards individuals, we must also think of tortures used by public officials, tortures used by people who, instead of catching criminals, become them themselves. If we allow us to turn a blind eye to such extremely sensitive matters, then there may be hundreds of such events as the case of Igor Stachowiak that Mr Andrzej Halicki referred to today. This has become an increasingly common phenomenon in the last two years, a dangerous phenomenon. We have to stop this absolutely. He recalled a few, several shocking examples of police behavior, which resulted in several deaths. Art. 40 of the Constitution states that no one must be subjected to torture or cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment. This is one of the fundamental principles of the entire system of human rights protection, which is clearly also embedded in international law. It should be mentioned here, for example, in the article. 3 the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, and the United Nations Convention ratified by Poland. Our Penal Code currently uses the term torture only in relation to a group of crimes against peace, humanity and war crimes. In our view, this regulation is insufficient. Because what are tortures? After all, it is nothing more than purposeful inflicting physical or mental pain on someone in a cruel way, especially by public officials. It shall constitute an Article. The 1st UN Convention already cited. That is why we are so positive about the parliamentary legislative initiative to amend the article. 246 and 247 Penal Code. In particular, the proposed amendments introduce the concept of torture in the context of the use of violence by public officials or by persons acting at their direction. It's not just a legitimate procedure, it's a procedure. It's necessary. This change is also due to the Committee's recommendation against the Tortures, which raised the legitimacy of including the definition of torture crime in the Polish legal order. This is of considerable practical importance for the application of the law itself, because it will distinguish between two distinct categories of criminal offences - on the one hand, violence by public officials and, on the other hand, acts of violence in a broad sense, committed simply by persons who do not act on behalf of the State. The proposed revision of the Penal Code is justified in substance, consistent, comprehensive and consistent with the principles of correct legislation. It strengthens the constitutional standard set out in Article. 40, as well as fulfilling our obligations under international law. This is reflected in the positive assessment of the Supreme Court, Mr. I also fully agree with the reasons put forward by the applicant - all possible initiatives must be taken that will prevent violence by public officials. Trust in institutions or guarantees of respect for human rights are key values for building state authority. Therefore, I would like to call on all the forces in Parliament to make a point on this project [[♪ The bell ♪]] work and not reject it simply because it is written by Members of the opposition. We're talking about safety, dignity and respect, about other people's lives. Mrs. Marshal! ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u376	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! It is very sad that such important laws, such as. Ladies and gentlemen! The Internet is a communication tool, which differs from traditional media, that anyone in this virtual world can be both the recipient of the message and the sender of the message. On the one hand, it gives citizens the opportunity to express their views freely, which is of course a very positive phenomenon, on the other hand, such freedom of expression combined with the sense of anonymity - because on the Internet we can be anonymous - it makes the Internet a breeding ground for hate speech that violates the fundamental civil rights and human dignity of specific people, all of us, because it concerns all of us, not only Members of the Modern Platform, Kukiz.15, Rights and Justice, and all citizens and citizens, regardless of where they live, what they do, what they call themselves. The present shape of both the Code of Civil Procedure and the Telecommunications Law does not fully agree to the possibility of using the Internet as a medium and to regulate the behaviour of citizens in this medium. We just can't keep up with what's going on in the virtual world, and it's worth working on. In view of the enormous scale of the problem of criminal proceedings in the area of hate speech, there are cases and very rare cases in our country, and the proposal for changes that we propose is intended to give all citizens, as well as NGOs, which is very important, concrete tools that protect each individual from hate speech individually. Currently, in civil proceedings, the entire burden of proof rests on the citizen and, in fact, at the beginning, the citizen is uphill, because, apart from proof of insult, the citizen is obliged to determine the person who insults him, namely his name, address of check-in or residence, if he insults the institution or even PESEL. In total, in the case of anonymous haters, and such are by far the most, the citizen or citizen, is simply powerless, because neither Internet operators nor internet service providers or Internet forums and social media operators they are not obliged to provide such information and without it the court proceedings do not comply with the formal conditions. In addition, there is a law on the protection of personal data and the Chief Inspector for Personal Data Protection has bound hands in this area and in fact it can be said that it works in favour of haters. In order to prevent this from changing so that there is no language degrengolada in the virtual world and to ensure a culture of public expression which should apply in a modern and democratic state of law, the current law should be amended and this law should protect citizens rather than the oppressors. I think that this is close to the Law and Justice, which has a majority in this Seym, which is primarily the direction of the Minister of Justice, and I think you just understand that. When we compare the state of law to date, our proposal provides for an extension of the catalogue of cases in which NGOs will be able, in their statutory tasks, to bring actions for citizens or to join such proceedings by adding them to the catalogue of cases for the protection of personal goods, because it does not work now. In addition, the proposal provides for the introduction of rules on the protection of personal property cases if they have been infringed through the Internet and the reason, i.e. the person who is harmed, does not know the personal data of the perpetrator of the infringement which are necessary to file the application. I'll tell you what the differences between the current state of law and the proposed state of law are. The fundamental difference between the current and the proposed legal situation is to allow citizens to bring actions without determining the defendant's personal data by introducing the so-called institutions. A blind lawsuit. Some time ago, the ombudsman said it would be a good solution. It would depend on the plaintiff's understanding of the three conditions. First of all, the reason, i.e. the person who would accuse, would be obliged to give a precise statement in a lawsuit that violates his personal property, including the URL under which these statements were posted, the dates and times of publication and the name of the user's profile or login. This requirement would allow the court to make a preliminary assessment as to whether the statements indicated by the person in question contain content which may affect his or her personal interests and, consequently, whether the action is actually justified or unfounded. The second thing is that a person who would make such a claim would also be obliged to file a statement in the action that both before and after the statements in question did not infringe the defendant's personal belongings, the accuracy of which would be assessed by the court already at the stage of the preliminary examination of the case. This condition is particularly important, since the specific procedure provided for by the proposed legislation should not be available in the event of mutual infringement of personal property by the author of the contested statement and by the person seeking judicial protection. It should also be borne in mind that the extremely unfavourable phenomenon observed in public space is the cases of repeated and mutual violations of the dignity of other people by Internet users. This is particularly the case during the exchange of opinions on web portals or social media. Therefore, in order to reduce the scale of this phenomenon, we believe that the possibility of using a specific procedure should be reserved only for people who themselves do not violate the personal property of other Internet users. Third point: the applicant would be required to file a statement in the application that he has attempted to notify the defendant via the Internet of his intention to bring an action against him or that such a notification was not possible. Ladies and gentlemen, one of you may say that this is an attempt on freedom of expression, and I would like to say that there is a fundamental difference between freedom of speech and freedom of expression and a speech of hatred that violates human personal interests, which violates dignity. One of the eminent thinkers - we often quote him here from this speaker - Alexis de Tocqueville, formulated one of the fundamental principles of the rule of law. Many times I have heard this statement from politicians in principle all options: human freedom ends where the freedom of another human being begins. A speech of hatred, ladies and gentlemen, is nothing more than an unauthorized attack on another man and stripping him of his rightful freedom and dignity. We all, as we sit here, all who may be listening to us, are or may become victims of hate speech, regardless of which party we are. The project we are presenting today is a project that is designed to protect our fundamental rights regardless of gender, religion, nationality, political views and anything else. This project is neither right-wing nor left-wing, because the politicians of all the groups here were victims of the heyth, and some of them are, unfortunately, even the oppressors in this area. This project is also a project that refers to our common values, which I confess, confesses Ryszard Petru, Mr Schetyn, even Mr Kaczyński... In times where we live now, in our divided country, in a divided society, let it be our common denominator, because every nation and every society, in order to be healthy and functioning well, must just have such a common denominator. Unfortunately, this project contains a lot of legal defects and I think I won't be able to talk about everyone for 5 minutes. First of all, it contains solutions for bringing an action without identifying the defendant. This constitutes a serious derogation from the general rules for initiating and conducting civil proceedings. It is unacceptable for a court to impose a statutory obligation to indicate the parties to the proceedings, in this case the defendant. The preliminary examination of the case by the same court makes it difficult for the case to be heard impartially in the same composition at a later stage of the proceedings. Admissibility of rejection due to obvious unfoundedness or mutual violation of personal property - then this is a substantive assessment. All grounds for rejection - completely different - are set out in Article. 199 c.p.c. There are no solutions in this draft concerning the situation where the court does not obtain data from the public telecommunications network operator as to the defendant or where the defendant's identity is wrongly identified. Any such change must take into account system solutions and those guarantees which allow for the recovery of claims under a given procedure. The plaintiff shall be required to designate the defendant in a lawsuit in accordance with Article. 126 § 1 c.p.c. This is a clear contradiction. The court is to determine the defendant on the passive side. This violates the principle of availability of a trial belonging to the plaintiff, violates the principle of adversariality. The Court of First Instance shall become the body involved in determining which parties the dispute is to be fought between. The Court of First Instance will first have to establish the substantive admissibility of the claims made by the claimant. The reason will be to avoid making statements that may indicate a violation of personal property on both sides. The review of the decision by complaint will not be a substantive review, it will be a decision at a secret meeting in the second instance. The mechanisms provided for in this project provide for the provision of a public telecommunications network operator or a provider of publicly available physical telecommunications services so-called. the point where the subscriber receives access to the public network, i.e. the device, the place. It should be noted that Article. 126 § 1 point 1 c.p.c. does not introduce any exceptions in this respect. According to the wording of the proposed Article. 505 § 2 point 2 c.p.c. in the action, the plaintiff is required to declare that the defendant's personal property has not been infringed either before or after the publication of the statements violating the plaintiff's personal interests. At the same time, the plaintiff is not obliged to indicate the defendant's personal data in a lawsuit. 2. Aren't such records contradictory internally? ParlaMint-PL_2017-09-27-sejm-48-1.u407	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party our position on the parliamentary draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code Act and the Telecommunications Law Act, print no. 1715. The present parliamentary proposal aims to strengthen citizens' legal protection in the area of the protection of personal property and in particular to facilitate citizens' civil redress in cases of breaches of personal property which occur via the Internet. The most important amendment requested by applicants is the introduction of the so-called. A blind lawsuit. Currently, a citizen who wishes to bring a case for the protection of personal property is obliged to indicate in the lawsuit, among others. the name of the defendant and his place of residence and address. The absence of indication of the above data is a formal lack of which non-compliance within a weekly period results in the recovery of the application. The proposed amendment reverses this principle. From now on, it will be in court, after a preliminary examination of whether the lawsuit is justified, to determine the identity of the Internet user who has been sued for violation of personal property. This solution is understandable in the context of the anonymity of the authors of comments on the Internet. Today, it is extremely difficult for a person whose goods have been violated to establish the identity of the perpetrator, and without it, it is impossible to prosecute his rights before a court. As a result, it can be said that the rights of this category of victims are not properly protected today. Another barrier faced by citizens seeking civil protection of personal goods is often the lack of professional knowledge necessary to bring an action and the considerable costs of legal proceedings. In order to remedy this, the project's authors intend to make it easier for citizens to handle personal goods by granting them the opportunity to benefit from the assistance provided by NGOs. Non-governmental organisations are to be given the power to bring actions for the protection of personal property and to join such proceedings. As a result, citizens will gain professional help in handling such cases. In the case of actions brought against citizens by non-governmental organisations, citizens will not be charged court fees, as these charges will be charged to the non-governmental organisation initiating the procedure each time. High Izbo! Today, this is one of the main demands that we raise in the debate on Polish judiciary. We want courts that will be closer to people, which will be accessible to them, not blocked by the wall of non-living laws and high court costs. The aid that NGOs could provide is invaluable to many victims. This record should be commended and supported. However, one might ask what about organisations that are ideologically defined one way or the other, extremely right-wing or extremely left-wing. Isn't it suddenly going to turn out that a violent political dispute on the Internet is going to spill over to the courts, pouring in a huge amount of lawsuits? We must take this into account in the course of further work on the project, but I would like to go back to the plaintiff's draft procedure for filing the application: the plaintiff would be obliged to file a statement in a lawsuit that both before and after the statements in question did not infringe the defendant's personal property, the accuracy of which would be assessed by the court already at the preliminary stage of the examination of the case on the basis of the statements of both parties attached to the action; this condition is particularly relevant in the assessment of the applicants. High Izbo! What if a reason, trying to clear itself of guilt, e.g. When he has offended someone himself before, will he present to the court a biased part of the internet discussion and not make statements incriminating to him? After all, today all this is easy to edit, even by deleting the comment. As I understand it, we will now require the courts to review the discussions directly on the portals and, if necessary, to obtain from the website administrators the archive records of the pages in order to reach the first unedited post factum version of the discussion. We all use the Internet today and we know what tricks some people can use in online discussions. High Izbo! The project should be considered worthy of attention, the protection of personal property of Internet users should be strengthened. However, further work needs to be taken into account in the draft preliminary procedure for assessing the action by the court and making appropriate clarifications. Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the club PSL request that the project be submitted for further work.
